这现象存在于种种的形态中，并非表明外面的世界变化了，而是观察这熟悉之景的人，其内心发生了重要而突然的变化，让他有“一日不见，如隔三秋”之感。牧师自己的意志，赫斯特的意志，还有两人之间萌生的命运，造成了这种改变。镇子还是原来的镇子，但林中归来，牧师已不是原来的牧师了。他或许会和向他打招呼的朋友们说，“我已经不是你们认识的那个人了！我把那个人丢在了那边的树林里，隐退到了一个秘密的山谷里，一棵长满青苔的树干旁，一条忧郁的小溪的不远处！去那里寻找你们的牧师吧，去看看，他那憔悴的身影，那消瘦的面庞，那苍白、沉重、爬满痛苦皱纹的额头，是不是像件被丢弃的衣服一样扔在那里！”他的朋友一定会继续对他说：“你就是那个人啊！”不过，恐怕是他们弄错了，而不是他。

Before Mr. Dimmesdale reached home, his inner man gave him other evidences of a revolution in the sphere of thought and feeling. In truth, nothing short of a total change of dynasty and moral code, in that interior kingdom, was adequate to account for the impulses now communicated to the unfortunate and startled minister. At every step he was incited to do some strange, wild, wicked thing or other, with a sense that it would be at once involuntary and intentional; in spite of himself, yet growing out of a profounder self than that which opposed the impulse. For instance, he met one of his own deacons. The good old man addressed him with the paternal affection and patriarchal privilege, which his venerable age, his upright and holy character, and his station in the Church, entitled him to use; and, conjoined with this, the deep, almost worshipping respect, which the minister's professional and private claims alike demanded. Never was there a more beautiful example of how the majesty of age and wisdom may comport with the obeisance and respect enjoined upon it, as from a lower social rank, and inferior order of endowment, towards a higher. Now, during a conversation of some two or three moments between the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale and this excellent and hoary-bearded deacon, it was only by the most careful self-control that the former could refrain from uttering certain blasphemous suggestions that rose into his mind, respecting the communion supper. He absolutely trembled and turned pale as ashes, lest his tongue should wag itself, in utterance of these horrible matters, and plead his own consent for so doing, without his having fairly given it. And, even with this terror in his heart, he could hardly avoid laughing, to imagine how the sanctified old patriarchal deacon would have been petrified by his minister's impiety! 

丁梅斯代尔回到家之前，内心的那个人又一次证明了，他的思想感情已经经历了一场蜕变。事实上，对不幸而惊惧的牧师来说，内心王国所传递给他的种种冲动，不亚于改朝换代，纲常全非。他每走一步，都想做些出奇、狂野、邪恶的事情。这念头时而不由自主，时而有意为之。那不是他自己的想法，而是发自更深处那反对这冲动的自我。比如说，他遇到了他的一位执事。那好心的老人用父亲般的慈爱和长老式的姿态，跟他打招呼。他已到了备受尊敬的年龄，品格正直，在教会颇有身份，才会如此。而与此相应的，老人对牧师怀有深切的、近乎崇拜的敬意——这是他的职业和个人品德所应赢得的。这是绝好的范例来说明：年龄与智慧的威严跟与之相应的遵从和敬意可以一致起来，社会地位低、天赋差的人对比他高的人也可以做得十分相称。这会儿，在丁梅斯代尔牧师先生和这位德高望重、胡须灰白的执事谈话的片刻，牧师小心地控制着自己，才没有把涌上心头的那番亵渎圣餐神明的话说出来。他确实颤抖了一下，面如土灰，生怕自己的舌头不经他认可，就自作主张地抖出那些可怕的事情。他虽然心存恐惧，但一想到这圣洁的老执事听到自己的不敬之言后会怎样得哑口无言，还是禁不住笑了出来。

Again, another incident of the same nature. Hurrying along the street, the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale encountered the eldest female member of his church; a most pious and exemplary old dame; poor, widowed, lonely, and with a heart as full of reminiscences about her dead husband and children, and her dead friends of long ago, as a burial-ground is full of storied gravestones. Yet all this, which would else have been such heavy sorrow, was made almost a solemn joy to her devout old soul, by religious consolations and the truths of Scripture, wherewith she had fed herself continually for more than thirty years. And, since Mr. Dimmesdale had taken her in charge, the good grandam's chief earthly comfort — which, unless it had been likewise a heavenly comfort, could have been none at all — was to meet her pastor, whether casually, or of set purpose, and be refreshed with a word of warm, fragrant, heaven-breathing Gospel truth, from his beloved lips, into her dulled, but rapturously attentive ear. But, on this occasion, up to the moment of putting his lips to the old woman's ear, Mr. Dimmesdale, as the great enemy of souls would have it, could recall no text of Scripture, nor aught else, except a brief, pithy, and, as it then appeared to him, unanswerable argument against the immortality of the human soul. The instilment thereof into her mind would probably have caused this aged sister to drop down dead at once, as by the effect of an intensely poisonous infusion. What he really did whisper, the minister could never afterwards recollect. There was, perhaps, a fortunate disorder in his utterance, which failed to impart any distinct idea to the good widow's comprehension, or which Providence interpreted after a method of its own. Assuredly, as the minister looked back, he beheld an expression of divine gratitude and ecstasy that seemed like the shine of the celestial city on her face, so wrinkled and ashy pale. 

还有另一件性质相同的事情。丁梅斯代尔牧师先生正在街上赶着路，遇到了最为年长的女教友，一个十分虔诚和模范的老妇人。她是个寡妇，贫穷又孤独，她的记忆就像满是名人墓碑的坟场，充满了对她已故丈夫、孩子以及朋友的怀念。三十多年来，她一直用宗教的慰藉和圣经的真理来充实自己。在此过程中，这原来沉重的悲伤变成了她那虔诚灵魂里肃穆的喜悦。自从丁梅斯代尔先生收她为教友以来，这位好心的老妈妈在世间唯一的安慰就是同牧师见面，随意地或是刻意地。如果这都不算是天国的安慰，那就没有什么称得上是了。她虽耳朵不好使，却乐意专心地听那可爱的双唇说出福音真谛，字句都带有温馨的天国气息，让她为之神清气爽。而现在，在他的嘴唇靠近老妈妈耳朵的那一刻，丁梅斯代尔先生如灵魂的大敌所愿，怎么也想不起《圣经》的经文了，只说一个反对灵魂不朽的观点，想不起任何其他话来。而这个简单概要的观点，在他当时看来是无可置辩的。这些话要是灌输给那上了年纪的女教友，她会像中了剧毒一样，立即倒地死去。他到底耳语了些什么，牧师后来怎么都想不起来了。也许多亏了他讲得语无伦次，或是上天用自己的方式做出了解释，那好心的寡妇没能清楚地理解他的意思。反正，当牧师回头看时，她的表情带着无比的感激和喜悦，仿佛是天国之光照到了她那满是皱纹的、灰白的脸。

Again a third instance. After parting from the old church-member, he met the youngest sister of them all. It was a maiden newly won — and won by the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale's own sermon, on the Sabbath after his vigil — to barter the transitory pleasures of the world for the heavenly hope, that was to assume brighter substance as life grew dark around her, and which would gild the utter gloom with final glory. She was fair and pure as a lily that had bloomed in Paradise. The minister knew well that he was himself enshrined within the stainless sanctity of her heart, which hung its snowy curtains about his image, imparting to religion the warmth of love, and to love a religious purity. Satan, that afternoon, had surely led the poor young girl away from her mother's side, and thrown her into the pathway of this sorely tempted, or — shall we not rather say? — this lost and desperate man. As she drew nigh, the arch-fiend whispered him to condense into small compass and drop into her tender bosom a germ of evil that would be sure to blossom darkly soon, and bear black fruit betimes. Such was his sense of power over this virgin soul, trusting him as she did, that the minister felt potent to blight all the field of innocence with but one wicked look, and develop all its opposite with but a word. So — with a mightier struggle than he had yet sustained — he held his Geneva cloak before his face, and hurried onward, making no sign of recognition, and leaving the young sister to digest his rudeness as she might. She ransacked her conscience — which was full of harmless little matters, like her pocket or her workbag — and took herself to task, poor thing! for a thousand imaginary faults; and went about her household duties with swollen eyelids the next morning. 

还有第三个例子。他离开老教友后，又遇到了他最年轻的女教友。这是新近才皈依的少女。丁梅斯代尔牧师先生在夜游后的第一个安息日讲道时，吸引了她。她要用现世短暂的快乐换取天国的希望。在她的生活变得黯淡时，那希望就愈发明亮，用最后的光辉为那彻底的黑暗镀上一层金。她如同在天堂盛开的百合花，娇好而纯洁。牧师十分清楚，在她那颗圣洁无暇的心中，自己被奉为神圣。那雪白的心灵帷幔垂盖在他的肖像上，为宗教注入了爱情的温暖，也为爱情注入了宗教的纯洁。那个下午，一定是撒旦把这可怜的女孩从她母亲身边引开了，并丢她到这心神不宁的，或者可以说，迷失而绝望的人的路上。她走进时，那魔王悄声要他缩小形体，在她那温柔的胸中投下邪恶的种子，那很快便会在暗处开花，到时结出黑色果实的种子。牧师能左右这少女灵魂的力量如此之大，对他的信任如此之深，让牧师觉得只要他不怀好意地看她一眼，她那无暇的心田就会枯萎；只要他说一个字，她纯洁的心灵就会走向反面。于是，在经历了前所未有的强烈内心斗争后，他用黑色的法衣遮住脸，急匆匆地向前走去，装出没认出她的模样，随那年轻的女教友去揣摩他的无礼。她细细搜寻着自己的良心——那里就如同衣袋和针线盒，充满了无害的小东西——想象出数以千计的错误来折磨自己。可怜的人儿！第二天一早，她又带着哭肿了的眼睛忙起了家务。

Before the minister had time to celebrate his victory over this last temptation, he was conscious of another impulse, more ludicrous, and almost as horrible. It was — we blush to tell it — it was to stop short in the road, and teach some very wicked words to a knot of little Puritan children who were playing there, and had but just begun to talk. Denying himself this freak, as unworthy of his cloth, he met a drunken seaman, one of the ship's crew from the Spanish Main. And, here, since he had so valiantly forborne all other wickedness, poor Mr. Dimmesdale longed, at least, to shake hands with the tarry blackguard, and recreate himself with a few improper jests, such as dissolute sailors so abound with, and a volley of good, round, solid, satisfactory, and heaven-defying oaths! It was not so much a better principle as partly his natural good taste, and still more his buckramed habit of clerical decorum, that carried him safely through the latter crisis. 

牧师还没来得及庆祝自己刚刚克服的诱惑，另一个更加可笑而几乎同样可怕的冲动，又潜入他的意识当中。我们说出来都脸红——他想在路上停下来，教那群正在玩耍的、才开始牙牙学语的清教徒小孩一些坏话。他否定了自己变态的行为，认为那与自己的法衣不相称。这时，他看见了一个来自拉美北部海域船队的、喝得烂醉的水手。这会儿，既然已经勇敢地克制了其他的邪恶，可怜的丁梅斯代尔先生想，至少可以和那些浑身沾满油污的粗人握握手，开几句坏坏的玩笑，就像那些放荡不羁的水手一样，并用一连串圆滑、实在、逗人乐的亵渎神明的诅咒寻寻开心！他之所以安全地度过这次难关，与其说他有什么更高的原则，还不如说在一定程度上，他天生品味高雅，更重要的是，那些教士礼仪已经深入他的习惯之中。

"What is it that haunts and tempts me thus?" cried the minister to himself, at length, pausing in the street, and striking his hand against his forehead. "Am I mad? Or am I given over utterly to the fiend? Did I make a contract with him in the forest, and sign it with my blood? And does he now summon me to its fulfilment, by suggesting the performance of every wickedness which his most foul imagination can conceive?"

“是什么在如此纠缠和诱惑着我啊？”最后，牧师停在街上，手拍额头，这样对自己喊道。“我是疯了吗？还是我已经全然受制于魔鬼？我是不是在树林里和他订下了契约，还用我的血签了名？现在他是否在传唤我去履行契约，让我去做他那最邪恶的想象力所想出的一切恶行？”

At the moment when the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale thus communed with himself, and struck his forehead with his hand, old Mistress Hibbins, the reputed witchlady, is said to have been passing by. She made a very grand appearance; having on a high head-dress, a rich gown of velvet, and a ruff done up with the famous yellow starch, of which Ann Turner, her especial friend, had taught her the secret, before this last good lady had been hanged for Sir Thomas Overbury's murder. Whether the witch had read the minister's thoughts or no, she came to a full stop, looked shrewdly into his face, smiled craftily, and — though little given to converse with clergymen — began a conversation. 

丁梅斯代尔牧师先生这样自言自语着，用力拍了拍额头。据说这时，那有名的妖婆西宾斯老太太正好经过。她是极为华丽地出现的——头戴高帽，身穿一袭华美的丝绒长袍，颈上围着用著名的黄糊精浆得笔挺的皱领。那糊精是她的挚友，安·特纳，在因谋杀托马斯·奥弗伯里而被绞之前，教给她的秘方。不论那妖婆是否读懂了牧师的心思，她停了下来，狡黠地盯着他的脸，坏笑了一声，开始和他攀谈——尽管没什么可讲的。

"So, reverend Sir, you have made a visit into the forest, " observed the witch-lady, nodding her high head-dress at him. "The next time, I pray you to allow me only a fair warning, and I shall be proud to bear you company. Without taking overmuch upon myself, my good word will go far towards gaining any strange gentleman a fair reception from yonder potentate you wot of!"

“尊敬的牧师先生，原来你去了趟树林。”老妖婆对他点了点戴着高帽的头，说道。“下次，你只消和我打个招呼，我会自豪地陪你去的。不是我吹牛，只要我美言一句，你知道的那位有权势的人，定会好好接待任何陌生的客人的！”

"I profess, madam, " answered the clergyman, with a grave obeisance, such as the lady's rank demanded, and his own good-breeding made imperative — "I profess, on my conscience and character, that I am utterly bewildered as touching the purport of your words! I went not into the forest to seek a potentate; neither do I, at any future time, design a visit thither, with a view to gaining the favour of such a personage. My one sufficient object was to greet that pious friend of mine, the Apostle Eliot, and rejoice with him over the many precious souls he hath won from heathendom!"

“说老实话，夫人，”牧师回答，出于那夫人的地位要求，还郑重其事地鞠了一躬——“说实话，我以我的良心和人格担保，实在是不明白您什么意思！我去林子可不是找权贵去的，以后也不会为了巴结权贵去那里的。我的唯一目的是去问候我一个虔诚的朋友，埃利奥特使徒。我为他从邪教那里赢回了那么多珍贵的灵魂而感到高兴！”

"Ha, ha, ha!" cackled the old witch-lady, still nodding her high head-dress at the minister. "Well, well, we must needs talk thus in the daytime! You carry it off like an old hand! But at midnight, and in the forest, we shall have other talk together!"

“哈，哈，哈！”老妖婆咯咯地笑了，依旧对着牧师点那戴着高帽的头。“好啦，好啦，我们大白天的只能这样讲话！这点你像个老手了！不过到了深更半夜，我们在树林里可得谈些别的！”

She passed on with her aged stateliness, but often turning back her head and smiling at him, like one willing to recognise a secret intimacy of connection. 

她摆出副年高德重的样子走开了，却总是回头冲他笑，像是要从中看出一段不可告人的私密关系似的。

"Have I then sold myself, " thought the minister, "to the fiend whom, if men say true, this yellow-starched and velveted old hag has chosen for her prince and master!"

“我当时是不是已经把自己出卖给那魔鬼了？”牧师思忖着，“如果人们说的是真的，这浆着黄领、穿着绒袍的老妖婆，早就选了她的王子和主子了！”

The wretched minister! He had made a bargain very like it! Tempted by a dream of happiness, he had yielded himself, with deliberate choice, as he had never done before, to what he knew was deadly sin. And the infectious poison of that sin had been thus rapidly diffused throughout his moral system. It had stupefied all blessed impulses, and awakened into vivid life the whole brotherhood of bad ones. Scorn, bitterness, unprovoked malignity, gratuitous desire of ill, ridicule of whatever was good and holy, all awoke, to tempt, even while they frightened him. And his encounter with old Mistress Hibbins, if it were a real incident, did but show his sympathy and fellowship with wicked mortals, and the world of perverted spirits. 

这可怜的牧师！他所做的交易和这很像啊！他被美梦所蛊惑，经过周密的抉择，屈从于明知的极恶——这是他不曾做过的。那具有传染性的罪恶之毒，就这样迅速地扩散到他的道德体系。它麻醉了一切神圣的冲动，唤醒了所有的恶念，使之变成了活跃的生命。轻蔑、狠毒、无缘由的歹意、不必要的恶念，都被唤醒了，去嘲弄所有善良和神圣的事物，虽说会吓到他，却仍要去蛊惑他。他若真的是和西宾斯老太太不期而遇的话，恰表明了，他已经跟那些邪恶的世人和堕落的灵魂同流合污了。

He had, by this time, reached his dwelling, on the edge of the burial-ground, and, hastening up the stairs, took refuge in his study. The minister was glad to have reached this shelter, without first betraying himself to the world by any of those strange and wicked eccentricities to which he had been continually impelled while passing through the streets. He entered the accustomed room, and looked around him on its books, its windows, its fireplace, and the tapestried comfort of the walls, with the same perception of strangeness that had haunted him throughout his walk from the forest-dell into the town, and thitherward. Here he had studied and written; here, gone through fast and vigil, and come forth half alive; here, striven to pray; here, borne a hundred thousand agonies! There was the Bible, in its rich old Hebrew, with Moses and the Prophets speaking to him, and God's voice through all! There, on the table, with the inky pen beside it, was an unfinished sermon, with a sentence broken in the midst, where his thoughts had ceased to gush out upon the page, two days before. He knew that it was himself, the thin and white-cheeked minister, who had done and suffered these things, and written thus far into the Election Sermon! But he seemed to stand apart, and eye this former self with scornful, pitying, but half-envious curiosity. That self was gone. Another man had returned out of the forest; a wiser one; with a knowledge of hidden mysteries which the simplicity of the former never could have reached. A bitter kind of knowledge that! 

这个时候，他已经到了自己在坟场边上的住所，快速地走上楼梯，躲到自己的书房里去了。牧师躲进了这庇护所，暗自高兴。这样他就无须向世人暴露那些一路上不断怂恿着他的离奇邪念了。他走进熟悉的房间，四下望了眼这里的书、窗户、壁炉和挂着壁毯的墙。从树林走到镇上的这一路，那种陌生感一直纠缠着他，此刻也依然存在。他曾在这里研习和写作；他曾在这里斋戒和夜祷，出来时已经半死不活了；他曾在这里努力祈祷；他曾在这里受尽折磨！这里有本《圣经》，用久远的希伯来语写着摩西和先知对他的训诫，上帝之音贯穿始终！那儿，在桌子上，在沾着墨水的鹅毛笔旁边，是一篇未完成的布道词。一个句子写到一半就断了，因为在两天前，他突然文思枯竭，写不下去了。他明白是他本人，这造成和承受了一切的牧师，这身体精瘦、面颊苍白的牧师，写了这些庆祝选举的布道文！但他似乎站得很远，带着轻蔑、怜悯，却又半怀着好奇心，审视着原先的自己。那样的自我不见了。另一个人从树林里出来了。他更加明智，带着隐秘的知识——这是前者简单的头脑所不能企及的。那知识是辛酸的！

While occupied with these reflections, a knock came at the door of the study, and the minister said, "Come in!" — not wholly devoid of an idea that he might behold an evil spirit. And so he did! It was old Roger Chillingworth that entered. The minister stood, white and speechless, with one hand on the Hebrew Scriptures, and the other spread upon his breast. 

他正这样想着，书房的门上传来一阵敲门声，牧师说，“进来吧！”——他并非没想到会看到一个邪恶的灵魂。他于是就进来了！进来的是老罗杰·奇林沃斯。牧师站在那里，面色苍白，与他相对无言，一手放在希伯来文的《圣经》上，一手捂着胸口。

"Welcome home, reverend Sir, " said the physician. "And how found you that godly man, the Apostle Eliot? But methinks, dear Sir, you look pale; as if the travel through the wilderness had been too sore for you. Will not my aid be requisite to put you in heart and strength to preach your Election Sermon?"

“欢迎回家，尊敬的牧师先生，”这医生说道。“你那位圣洁的埃利奥特使徒可好啊？可我觉得你看上去有些苍白，亲爱的先生，大概是野林里路途劳累吧。要不要我帮你恢复下身心，好让你更好地在庆祝选举中讲道呢？”

"Nay, I think not so, " rejoined the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale. "My journey, and the sight of the holy Apostle yonder, and the free air which I have breathed, have done me good, after so long confinement in my study. I think to need no more of your drugs, my kind physician, good though they be, and administered by a friendly hand."

“不，我看不必了。”丁梅斯代尔牧师先生接道。“我这一趟见到了那圣洁的使徒，呼吸了自由的空气，对我大有好处啊，之前闷在书房里的时间太长了。虽说药很好，又是友好之手给的，但我想我还是不需要你的药了，好心的医生。”

All this time, Roger Chillingworth was looking at the minister with the grave and intent regard of a physician towards his patient. But, in spite of this outward show, the latter was almost convinced of the old man's knowledge, or, at least, his confident suspicion, with respect to his own interview with Hester Prynne. The physician knew then, that, in the minister's regard, he was no longer a trusted friend, but his bitterest enemy. So much being known, it would appear natural that a part of it should be expressed. It is singular, however, how long a time often passes before words embody things; and with what security two persons, who choose to avoid a certain subject, may approach its very verge, and retire without disturbing it. Thus, the minister felt no apprehension that Roger Chillingworth would touch, in express words, upon the real position which they sustained towards one another. Yet did the physician, in his dark way, creep frightfully near the secret. 

这会儿，罗杰·奇林沃斯始终用医生对病人的那种严肃和专注望着牧师。不过，牧师表面虽如此，但想到他和赫斯特·普林的见面，心里几乎确信老人已经知道了，或者至少已在暗中怀疑他了。那时医生就知道，在牧师看来，他已经不再是一个值得信任的朋友，而是死对头了。他知道了这些，很自然会有所流露。而奇妙的是，真话总在长时间后才会出现。二人为了避免一些话题，总是小心翼翼地触到边缘，又退缩回去，才不致点破。这样，牧师觉得，罗杰·奇林沃斯不会公然说出两人互相维持的真实地位。不过那医生确实用卑鄙的手段，不安地向秘密爬近。

"Were it not better, " said he, "that you use my poor skill to-night? Verily, dear Sir, we must take pains to make you strong and vigorous for this occasion of the Election discourse. The people look for great things from you; apprehending that another year may come about, and find their pastor gone."

“今晚你接受我卑微的医术，不是更好吗？”他说，“真的，亲爱的先生，我们要努力让你精力充沛，以便参加庆祝选举的宣讲。人们对你有很大的期望，担心新年一到，他们的牧师就不在了。”

"Yea, to another world, " replied the minister, with pious resignation. "Heaven grant it be a better one; for, in good sooth, I hardly think to tarry with my flock through the flitting seasons of another year! But, touching your medicine, kind Sir, in my present frame of body, I need it not."

“是啊，去往另一个世界。”牧师带着听天由命的神气回答道。“上天保佑那是个更好的世界吧。老实讲，我不觉得自己可以和教民们共度那转瞬即逝的来年！不过，至于你的药，好心的先生，就我目前的身体状况，我并不需要了。”

"I joy to hear it, " answered the physician. "It may be that my remedies, so long administered in vain, begin now to take due effect. Happy man were I, and well deserving of New England's gratitude, could I achieve this cure!"

“很高兴听到这个。”医生回答。“我的药方这么长时间不起作用，也许现在开始显效了。如果我真的治好了你的病，我会很高兴的，也对得起新英格兰的感激之情了。”

"I thank you from my heart, most watchful friend, " said the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale, with a solemn smile. "I thank you, and can but requite your good deeds with my prayers."

“我真心地谢谢你，尽心的朋友。”丁梅斯代尔牧师先生郑重地笑了下，说道。“谢谢你，而我也只能用祈祷来回报你的善行了。”

"A good man's prayers are golden recompense!" rejoined old Roger Chillingworth, as he took his leave. "Yea, they are the current gold coin of the New Jerusalem, with the King's own mintmark on them!"

“一个好人的祈祷如用黄金作酬谢！”老罗杰·奇林沃斯一边后退，一边接口道。“是啊，那是新耶路撒冷通用的金币，上面铸着上帝的头像！”

Left alone, the minister summoned a servant of the house, and requested food, which, being set before him, he ate with ravenous appetite. Then, flinging the already written pages of the Election Sermon into the fire, he forthwith began another, which he wrote with such an impulsive flow of thought and emotion, that he fancied himself inspired; and only wondered that Heaven should see fit to transmit the grand and solemn music of its oracles through so foul an organ-pipe as he. However, leaving that mystery to solve itself, or go unsolved forever, he drove his task onward, with earnest haste and ecstasy. Thus the night fled away, as if it were winged steed, and he careering on it; morning came, and peeped, blushing, through the curtains; and at last sunrise threw a golden beam into the study and laid it right across the minister's bedazzled eyes. There he was, with the pen still between his fingers, and a vast, immeasurable tract of written space behind him! 

只剩一个人后，牧师唤来屋子里的一个仆人，叫了些食物。食物一端上来，他便狼吞虎咽地吃起来。之后，他把写好的庆祝选举的布道文扔进了炉火，立马提笔另写。他觉得自己受到了神的启示，思绪源源不断地涌到笔尖。只是他不明白，上天为何把那肃穆庄严的神谕之乐，传递给他这么一件肮脏的管风琴。不过，谜底自己解开也好，永远存在也罢，他继续完成他的使命，专注而欣喜地奋笔疾书。那一夜如插翅的骏马般飞奔而过，而他就骑在马背上。清晨来了，从那窗帘外偷偷张望，面颊绯红。终于，旭日将金色的光芒投射进了书房，正好照到牧师迷离的眼睛上。他的指间依旧夹着鹅毛笔，已经洋洋洒洒地写了一大篇文字了！

# Chapter 21THE NEW ENGLAND HOLIDAY

**第二十一章 新英格兰的节日**

Betimes in the morning of the day on which the new Governor was to receive his office at the hands of the people Hester Prynne and little Pearl came into the market-place. It was already thronged with the craftsmen and other plebeian inhabitants of the town, in considerable numbers; among whom, likewise, were many rough figures, whose attire of deerskins marked them as belonging to some of the forest settlements, which surrounded the little metropolis of the colony. 

那天是新总督上任的日子，赫斯特·普林和小珠儿一早就来到了集市上。那里已经人山人海，挤满了工匠和其他镇上的百姓，其中也有一些粗人。他们身上的鹿皮大衣表明，他们是来自殖民地城市外围的林中居民。

On this public holiday, as on all other occasions, for seven years past, Hester was clad in a garment of coarse grey cloth. Not more by its hue than by some indescribable peculiarity in its fashion, it had the effect of making her fade personally out of sight and outline; while, again, the scarlet letter brought her back from this twilight indistinctness and revealed her under the moral aspect of its own illumination. Her face, so long familiar to the townspeople, showed the marble quietude which they were accustomed to behold there. It was like a mask; or, rather, like the frozen calmness of a dead woman's features; owing this dreary resemblance to the fact that Hester was actually dead, in respect to any claim of sympathy, and had departed out of the world, with which she still seemed to mingle. 

赫斯特在这公共的节日里，和在这过去七年的其他场合一样，穿着粗糙的灰布袍子。这衣服的颜色，特别是那不可名状的独特款式，让她显得不那么引人注目。然而，又是那红字把她从昏暗难辨的境地中带了出来，置于它自身的道德光照之中。她那已经被人们熟悉的面孔，透着大理石般的肃穆。那脸像一张面具，像一张死去的女人的脸，僵硬而安静。我们作这样沉闷的类比，是因为她虽说混迹于世，实际上却与之背离，无权博得同情。

It might be, on this one day, that there was an expression unseen before, nor, indeed, vivid enough to be detected, now; unless some preternaturally gifted observer should have first read the heart, and have afterwards sought a corresponding development in the countenance and mien. Such a spiritual seer might have conceived that, after sustaining the gaze of the multitude through seven miserable years as a necessity, a penance, and something which it was a stern religion to endure, she now, for one last time more, encountered it freely and voluntarily, in order to convert what had so long been agony into a kind of triumph. "Look your last on the scarlet letter and its wearer!" — the people's victim and life-long bond-slave, as they fancied her, might say to them. "Yet a little while, and she will be beyond your reach! A few hours longer, and the deep, mysterious ocean will quench and hide for ever the symbol which ye have caused to burn upon her bosom!" Nor were it an inconsistency too improbable to be assigned to human nature, should we suppose a feeling of regret in Hester's mind, at the moment when she was about to win her freedom from the pain which had been thus deeply incorporated with her being. Might there not be an irresistible desire to quaff a last, long, breathless draught of the cup of wormwood and aloes, with which nearly all her years of womanhood had been perpetually flavoured? The wine of life, henceforth to be presented to her lips, must be indeed rich, delicious, and exhilarating, in its chased and golden beaker; or else leave an inevitable and weary languor, after the lees of bitterness wherewith she had been drugged, as with a cordial of intensest potency. 

这一天，她或许有种前所未有的表情，不过此刻确实不是清晰可查。除非观察者才天赋异能，首先可以洞察她的内心，之后才能在她的表情和举止上寻到些相应的变化。这样的精神洞察者或许可以想象：这七年，她痛苦地忍受众人异样的眼光，把这当成一种必须、一种惩罚和一种严格的宗教来忍受；如今她最后一次，自在自觉地面对这些目光，要把长时间的苦难转化成一种胜利。“最后看一眼那红字和佩戴着它的人吧！”她，这人们眼中的受害者和终身奴仆，会这样告诉他们。“再过一会儿，她就不在你们伸手可及之处了！几个小时以后，你们放在她胸口的燃烧的徽记，将永远沉没在深不可测的海洋里！”假如我们猜测，在即将获得自由、摆脱那深入生命的痛苦时，赫斯特心生悔意，这种不一致也不是完全有悖于人性。她身为妇人的这些年，一直品尝着苦艾和芦荟的苦涩，难道现在她就不能有难抑的欲望，想要长长地一口气吞下那杯苦水吗？今后举到她唇边的生命之酒，一定是盛在那金色的雕花高脚杯里，醇厚、馥郁和令人陶醉的。不然，她在喝惯了这最烈的、兴奋剂式的苦酒渣后，免不了会倦怠。

Pearl was decked out with airy gayety. It would have been impossible to guess that this bright and sunny apparition owed its existence to the shape of gloomy grey; or that a fancy, at once so gorgeous and so delicate as must have been requisite to contrive the child's apparel, was the same that had achieved a task perhaps more difficult, in imparting so distinct a peculiarity to Hester's simple robe. The dress, so proper was it to little Pearl, seemed an effluence, or inevitable development and outward manifestation of her character, no more to be separated from her than the many-hued brilliancy from a butterfly's wing or the painted glory from the leaf of a bright flower. As with these, so with the child; her garb was all of one idea with her nature. On this eventful day, moreover, there was a certain singular inquietude and excitement in her mood, resembling nothing so much as the shimmer of a diamond, that sparkles and flashes with the varied throbbings of the breast on which it is displayed. Children have always a sympathy in the agitations of those connected with them; always, especially, a sense of any trouble or impending revolution, of whatever kind, in domestic circumstances; and therefore Pearl, who was the gem on her mother's unquiet bosom, betrayed, by the very dance of her spirits, the emotions which none could detect in the marble passiveness of Hester's brow. 

珠儿被打扮得赏心悦目。人们怎么都猜不到，这阳光般明媚的精灵竟来自那阴暗之躯；或者说，人们惊讶于孩子身上那华丽精美的服饰设计，与赫斯特那以简朴为突出特点的裙子——这个任务或许更困难些——竟是出自同一个人之手。小珠儿穿的裙子恰到好处，俨然是个性的流露，或是必然的发展和外在表现，就像蝴蝶翅膀上的绚丽多彩，或者是灿烂花朵上花瓣的鲜艳光辉那样，不能与本体分割开来。衣裙之于孩子，也是一样的，与她的本性浑然一体。这是重大的一天，更重要的是，她的心情有种异样的不安和兴奋，像极了钻石，随着胸口不同节奏的悸动闪闪发光。孩子们，特别是当家里出现困难或变动临近时，总能反映同与他们相关之人的情绪波动。因此，珠儿作为悬挂在母亲那不安胸口的一块宝石，她精神的舞动，暴露了在赫斯特那大理石般平静的额头上所不能觉察的情感。

This effervescence made her flit with a birdlike movement, rather than walk by her mother's side. She broke continually into shouts of a wild, inarticulate, and sometimes piercing music. When they reached the market-place, she became still more restless, on perceiving the stir and bustle that enlivened the spot; for it was usually more like the broad and lonesome green before a village meeting-house, than the centre of a town's business. 

这种欢跃之情使她像小鸟一样轻快活泼地蹦蹦跳跳，而不是安分地走在母亲身边。她不时地狂呼乱叫，不知所云，有时还发出刺耳的叫声。她们到了集市，她一见到那使整个地方活跃起来的喧闹气氛，就变得更加不安分了。因为，平日里，这儿与其说是镇里的商业中心，不如说是村会所前宽广寂静的绿地。

"Why, what is this, mother?" cried she. "Wherefore have all the people left their work today? Is it a play-day for the whole world? See, there is the blacksmith! He has washed his sooty face, and put on his Sabbath-day clothes and looks as if he would gladly be merry, if any kind body would only teach him how! And there is Master Brackett, the old jailer, nodding and smiling at me. Why does he do so, mother?"

“咦，这是什么，妈妈？”她叫道。“大家今天为什么都不干活了呀？今天是全世界的游玩日吗？看，铁匠在那儿！他洗掉了脸上的煤灰，穿上了安息日的衣服，看上去很想痛痛快快玩一场——要是有人能教教他的话。那位老狱吏布拉克特先生正朝我点头微笑呢。他为什么要这样呢，妈妈？”

"He remembers thee a little babe, my child, " answered Hester. 

“他记忆里你还是个小娃娃呢，我的孩子。”赫斯特回答。

"He should not nod and smile at me, for all that — the black, grim, ugly-eyed old man!" said Pearl. "He may nod at thee, if he will; for thou art clad in grey, and wearest the scarlet letter. But see, mother, how many faces of strange people, and Indians among them, and sailors! What have they all come to do, here in the market-place?"

“他才不会因为这个向我点头，还冲着我笑呢——那又黑又吓人，眼睛丑丑的老头！”珠儿说。“他要是愿意，倒是会向你点头的，因为你穿着一身灰色，还戴着那红字。可是，妈妈你瞧，这么多陌生的面孔，有印第安人，还有水手呢！他们来这市场上做什么呢？”

"They wait to see the procession pass, " said Hester. "For the Governor and the magistrates are to go by, and the ministers, and all the great people and good people, with the music and the soldiers marching before them."

“他们在等着看游行队伍经过呀。”赫斯特说，“因为总督、长官们、牧师们、所有伟大的人和好心的人要经过这儿，前面还会有乐队和军队开路。”

"And will the minister be there?" asked Pearl. "And will he hold out both his hands to me, as when thou ledst me to him from the brook-side?"

“那个牧师会在里面吗？”珠儿问。“他会对我伸开双手吗，就像你从小溪边领我见他的那次？”

"He will be there, child, " answered her mother. "But he will not greet thee today; nor must thou greet him."

“他会在那里的，孩子。”母亲回答。“但是他今天不会和你打招呼，你也不许和他打招呼。”

"What a strange, sad man is he!" said the child, as if speaking partly to herself. "In the dark night-time he calls us to him, and holds thy hand and mine, as when we stood with him on the scaffold yonder. And in the deep forest, where only the old trees can hear, and the strip of sky see it, he talks with thee, sitting on a heap of moss! And he kisses my forehead, too, so that the little brook would hardly wash it off! But here, in the sunny day, and among all the people, he knows us not; nor must we know him! A strange, sad man is he, with his hand always over his heart!"

“他真是个奇怪而可怜的人！”孩子说，有点像是自言自语。“在那漆黑的夜里，他叫我们去他那里，握着我们俩的手，陪他站在那刑台上。在深深的林子里，在那只有古树能听见、只有一线青天可以看见的地方，他和你坐在青苔堆上讲话！他还亲了亲我的额头，连溪水都洗不掉啦！可是在这里，在这艳阳天，在这么多人中间，他却不认识我们了，我们也不该认识他！那奇怪可怜的人啊，总是用手捂着心口！”

"Be quiet, Pearl! Thou understandest not these things, " said her mother. "Think not now of the minister, but look about thee, and see how cheery is everybody's face today. The children have come from their schools, and the grown people from their workshops and their fields, on purpose to be happy. For today a new man is beginning to rule over them; and so — as has been the custom of mankind ever since a nation was first gathered — they make merry and rejoice; as if a good and golden year were at length to pass over the poor old world!"

“安静点，珠儿！你不明白的。”她母亲说。“现在别想那牧师，看看你周围，大家的脸上都是多么高兴啊。孩子们从学校出来了，大人们从自己的工厂和田地里出来了，就是为了高兴高兴嘛。因为今天有一个新人来管理他们了，所以他们要像第一个国家成立时人们做的一样，欢天喜地一番，就像那穷兮兮的旧世界终于要有个黄金般的好年景了！”

It was as Hester said, in regard to the unwonted jollity that brightened the faces of the people. Into this festal season of the year — as it already was, and continued to be during the greater part of two centuries — the Puritans compressed whatever mirth and public joy they deemed allowable to human infirmity; thereby so far dispelling the customary cloud, that, for the space of a single holiday, they appeared scarcely more grave than most other communities at a period of general affliction. 

正如赫斯特所讲，人们脸上确实闪耀着不常见的欢愉。清教徒们把自认为人类的弱点所能容忍的欢乐和公共的喜庆，全都压缩在一年中这一喜庆的季节——过去如此，随后两个世纪的大部分年月依旧如此。因此，他们拨开积年的阴霾，在这仅有的节日里，他们的神情才比别处居民在苦难时刻的神情要少些沉重。

But we perhaps exaggerate the grey or sable tinge, which undoubtedly characterised the mood and manners of the age. The persons now in the market-place of Boston had not been born to an inheritance of Puritanic gloom. They were native Englishmen, whose fathers had lived in the sunny richness of the Elizabethan epoch; a time when the life of England, viewed as one great mass, would appear to have been as stately, magnificent, and joyous, as the world has ever witnessed. Had they followed their hereditary taste, the New England settlers would have illustrated all events of public importance by bonfires, banquets, pageantries, and processions. Nor would it have been impracticable, in the observance of majestic ceremonies, to combine mirthful recreation with solemnity, and give, as it were, a grotesque and brilliant embroidery to the great robe of state, which a nation, at such festivals, puts on. There was some shadow of an attempt of this kind in the mode of celebrating the day on which the political year of the colony commenced. The dim reflection of a remembered splendour, a colourless and manifold diluted repetition of what they had beheld in proud old London — we will not say at a royal coronation, but at a Lord Mayor's show — might be traced in the customs which our forefathers instituted, with reference to the annual installation of magistrates. The fathers and founders of the commonwealth — the statesman, the priest, and the soldier — deemed it a duty then to assume the outward state and majesty, which, in accordance with antique style, was looked upon as the proper garb of public or social eminence. All came forth, to move in procession before the people's eye, and thus impart a needed dignity to the simple framework of a government so newly constructed. 

这灰暗的色调无疑是当年心情和举止的特征，但我们也许夸张了一些。波士顿集市上的这些居民，并不是生来就遗传了清教的阴郁。他们生在英国，父辈们生活在富足明媚的伊丽莎白时代。从大体上看，那时英国人的生活显得高贵华丽和充满欢乐，这在世界上前所未有。如果新英格兰的定居者遵循传统的品位，就会用篝火、宴会、表演和游行来装点一切重大的公共活动。在隆重的典礼仪式中，把欢娱和庄重结合起来，用图案怪异、闪闪发亮的刺绣来装饰在这种节日才穿的国袍，也不会是不切实际的。在殖民地刚成立那年的庆典中，尚留有这种意图的影子。在我们祖先所制定的每年一度的执政官就职仪式里——我们且不谈国王的加冕典礼，只是讲市长大人的就职仪式——还隐约可见记忆中的光辉。那昔日伦敦的骄傲，经多次冲刷已经褪色。那时我们合众国的先辈和奠基者们——那些政治家，牧师和军人——把外表的庄严威武视为责任。依照古时的作风，这种打扮正是社会贤达人士的恰当装束。他们在人们眼前整队而行，为新建立的、机构精简的政府获取所需的威信。

Then, too, the people were countenanced, if not encouraged, in relaxing the severe and close application to their various modes of rugged industry, which, at all other times, seemed of the same piece and material with their religion. 

平时，人们视各种勤俭的生活方式如宗教教义，严格遵守。而在这种时候，人们即使没有受到鼓励，也被获准，放松这些准则。

Here, it is true, were none of the appliances which popular merriment would so readily have found in the England of Elizabeth's time, or that of James; no rude shows of a theatrical kind; no minstrel, with his harp and legendary ballad, nor gleeman, with an ape dancing to his music; no juggler, with his tricks of mimic witchcraft; no Merry Andrew, to stir up the multitude with jests, perhaps hundreds of years old, but still effective, by their appeals to the very broadest sources of mirthful sympathy. 

诚然，伊丽莎白时代或詹姆斯时代在英国比比皆是的通俗娱乐设施，在这里是没有的。这里也没有粗俗的剧场演出，没有拉着竖琴、唱着神秘歌谣的吟游诗人，没有奏着音乐耍猴的江湖人士，没有变戏法的民间艺人，没有逗人们开心的“快乐的安德鲁”——他们说的那些笑话都已流传了上百年，却仍能广泛地感染大家的快乐因子，使人发笑。

All such professors of the several branches of jocularity would have been sternly repressed, not only by the rigid discipline of law, but by the general sentiment which gives law its vitality. Not the less, however, the great, honest face of the people smiled, grimly, perhaps, but widely too. Nor were sports wanting, such as the colonists had witnessed, and shared in, long ago, at the country fairs and on the village-greens of England; and which it was thought well to keep alive on this new soil, for the sake of the courage and manliness that were essential in them. Wrestling-matches, in the different fashions of Cornwall and Devonshire, were seen here and there about the market-place; in one corner there was a friendly bout at quarterstaff; and — what attracted most interest of all — on the platform of the pillory, already so noted in our pages, two masters of defence were commencing an exhibition with the buckler and broadsword. But, much to the disappointment of the crowd, this latter business was broken off by the interposition of the town beadle, who had no idea of permitting the majesty of the law to be violated by such an abuse of one of its consecrated places. 

所有这些逗趣行家们，不仅被僵化的法律规定严格限制着，也被赋予法律以效力的公众情感所厌恶。然而，大众真诚的脸上依旧带着笑容，笑得夸张而不太自然。就像移民们很久以前在英国农村集市和草地上看到的那样，这里不乏竞技活动。考虑到其核心的勇气和阳刚精神，人们觉得应该在这片新大陆上加以保留。在市场上，到处能见到康沃尔郡和德文郡的种种摔跤比赛。在某一角落，有用铁头木棒作武器的友谊赛。最吸引人的是，在书中已多次提到过的那个刑台上，两位手持盾牌和宽剑的武士正开始一场公开表演。但令人扫兴的是，后者因镇上差役的干涉而中断。他们觉得，滥用这祭献之地侵犯了法律的威严，不能容忍。

It may not be too much to affirm, on the whole (the people being then in the first stages of joyless deportment, and the offspring of sires who had known how to be merry, in their day), that they would compare favourably, in point of holiday keeping, with their descendants, even at so long an interval as ourselves. Their immediate posterity, the generation next to the early emigrants, wore the blackest shade of Puritanism, and so darkened the national visage with it, that all the subsequent years have not sufficed to clear it up. We have yet to learn again the forgotten art of gayety. 

总体上，我们可以说：这些人——当时他们刚刚过上无欢乐活动的日子，而他们的父辈尚且知道如何行乐——比起他们的子孙，甚至是我们这些与其相隔甚久的人们，在过节方面算是懂得快活的了。早期移民的后代，也就是他们的下一代，披上了最深的清教阴影，令国家的面貌黯淡无光，以致随后的几年都不足以消除那种阴霾。我们只得再学一学那遗忘了的快乐之法。

The picture of human life in the market-place, though its general tint was the sad grey, brown, or black of the English emigrants, was yet enlivened by some diversity of hue. A party of Indians — in their savage finery of curiously embroidered deer-skin robes, wampum-belts, red and yellow ochre, and feathers, and armed with the bow and arrow and stone-headed spear — stood apart, with countenances of inflexible gravity, beyond what even the Puritan aspect could attain. Nor, wild as were these painted barbarians, were they the wildest feature of the scene. This distinction could more justly be claimed by some mariners — a part of the crew of the vessel from the Spanish Main — who had come ashore to see the humours of Election Day. They were rough-looking desperadoes, with sun-blackened faces, and an immensity of beard; their wide, short trousers were confined about the waist by belts, often clasped with a rough plate of gold, and sustaining always a long knife, and, in some instances, a sword. From beneath their broad-brimmed hats of palm-leaf, gleamed eyes which, even in good-nature and merriment, had a kind of animal ferocity. They transgressed, without fear or scruple, the rules of behaviour that were binding on all others; smoking tobacco under the beadle's very nose, although each whiff would have cost a townsman a shilling; and quaffing, at their pleasure, draughts of wine or aqua-vit from pocket-flasks, which they freely tendered to the gaping crowd around them. It remarkably characterised the incomplete morality of the age, rigid as we call it, that a license was allowed the seafaring class, not merely for their freaks on shore, but for far more desperate deeds on their proper element. The sailor of that day would go near to be arraigned as a pirate in our own. There could be little doubt, for instance, that this very ship's crew, though no unfavourable specimens of the nautical brotherhood, had been guilty, as we should phrase it, of depredations on the Spanish commerce, such as would have perilled all their necks in a modern court of justice. 

市场上这幅人生之景，虽带着英国移民那阴郁的灰色、褐色或黑色基调，却也因某些斑斓之色而彰显活力。一拨印第安人站在一旁，穿着野蛮人绣着奇形怪状图案的华丽鹿皮大衣，系着贝壳串成的腰带，带着红色和黄色赭石及羽毛做成的点缀，挎着弓箭，手执石尖长矛；那僵硬而严肃的面部表情，连清教徒都不可企及。但这些周身花花绿绿的粗人，还不是当场最野蛮的。更符合这特征的是那些水手，他们来自拉美北部海域船队，上岸来看庆祝选举日的热闹。他们是长相粗野的亡命之徒，脸被太阳晒得黝黑，胡子浓密。他们又肥又短的裤子用带子系在腰上，扣着粗糙的金片，上面总是插着一柄长矛或短剑。宽边棕榈叶帽子下的那双眼睛，即使在心情好、兴致高的时候，也透着兽性的凶光。他们肆无忌惮地违反束缚众人的行为准则：尽管每吸上一口都会被罚款一先令，他们还是公然在差役的鼻子底下吸烟；他们随心所欲地从衣袋里掏出酒瓶，大口地喝着葡萄酒或是烈性酒，还随意地递给周围目瞪口呆的人们。这突出地表现了那个年代残缺的道德标准。我们认为它严格死板，它却对浪迹海洋的人们网开一面，不仅容忍他们在岸上为所欲为，而且听凭他们在自己的天地里更加地无法无天。那时的水手近乎可以被视为当下的海盗。以这个船队为例，他们虽然并没有在航海同胞中臭名昭著，却抢劫了西班牙商船队；按我们的话说，他们几乎有无可置疑的罪行，在今天的法庭上，大概会人头不保吧。

But the sea in those old times heaved, swelled, and foamed, very much at its own will, or subject only to the tempestuous wind, with hardly any attempts at regulation by human law. The buccaneer on the wave might relinquish his calling, and become at once, if he chose, a man of probity and piety on land; nor, even in the full career of his reckless life, was he regarded as a personage with whom it was disreputable to traffic, or casually associate. Thus, the Puritan elders, in their black cloaks, starched bands, and steeple-crowned hats, smiled not unbenignantly at the clamour and rude deportment of these jolly seafaring men; and it excited neither surprise nor animadversion when so reputable a citizen as old Roger Chillingworth, the physician, was seen to enter the market-place in close and familiar talk with the commander of the questionable vessel. 

但那时的大海，或是波涛起伏，掀浪卷沫，肆意而为之，或是仅仅臣服于狂风暴雨，无意受制于人类法律。如果那些在风口浪尖上的海盗们愿意，他们可以洗手不干，马上成为岸上一名正直虔诚的君子。即使他们一辈子都肆意胡为，人们也不会耻于与他们交往。因此，那些穿着黑斗篷，系着浆好的皱领，戴着尖顶高帽的老一辈清教徒们，对于水手们快乐的喧闹和粗野的举止，反倒报以仁慈的微笑。看到老罗杰·奇林沃斯这样德高望重的居民兼医生走进市场，和那艘形迹可疑之船的船长亲密交谈，人们并没有惊讶，也没有议论纷纷。

The latter was by far the most showy and gallant figure, so far as apparel went, anywhere to be seen among the multitude. He wore a profusion of ribbons on his garment, and gold lace on his hat, which was also encircled by a gold chain and surmounted with a feather. There was a sword at his side, and a sword-cut on his forehead, which, by the arrangement of his hair, he seemed anxious rather to display than hide. A landsman could hardly have worn this garb and shown this face, and worn and shown them both with such a galliard air, without undergoing stern question before a magistrate, and probably incurring fine or imprisonment, or perhaps an exhibition in the stocks. As regarded the shipmaster, however, all was looked upon as pertaining to the character, as to a fish his glistening scales. 

那位船长无论出现在人群中的什么地方，都算是衣着最高调华丽的。他的衣服上有很多缎带，帽子上装点着金色蕾丝，镶着金边，帽顶上还插着根羽毛。他肋下佩着利剑，额头上有剑伤。从头发的样式看来，他似乎急于显露这疤痕，而不是加以掩盖。陆地上的人要是穿着这一身行头，摆着这幅尊容，得意洋洋地招摇过市，免不了被传唤到长官跟前接受严厉的审问，甚至还会被处以罚金或判以监禁，或是枷号示众。而对于这位船长而言，一切都像闪闪鱼鳞之于鱼那样，契合他的身份。

After parting from the physician, the commander of the Bristol ship strolled idly through the market-place; until, happening to approach the spot where Hester Prynne was standing, he appeared to recognise, and did not hesitate to address her. As was usually the case wherever Hester stood, a small vacant area — a sort of magic circle — had formed itself about her, into which, though the people were elbowing one another at a little distance, none ventured, or felt disposed to intrude. It was a forcible type of the moral solitude in which the scarlet letter enveloped its fated wearer; partly by her own reserve, and partly by the instinctive, though no longer so unkindly, withdrawal of her fellow-creatures. Now, if never before, it answered a good purpose by enabling Hester and the seaman to speak together without the risk of being overheard; and so changed was Hester Prynne's repute before the public, that the matron in town most eminent for rigid morality could not have held such intercourse with less result of scandal than herself. 

离开医生之后，这位准备驶往布里斯托尔的船长，悠闲地穿过市场，正好走近赫斯特·普林站着的地方，好像认出了她，就径直上去打招呼。一如往常，不论赫斯特站在哪里，身边总会留出如魔力圈般的小片空地——人们再怎么摩肩接踵，也不敢或是不愿进入。部分由于她自己的回避，部分由于她的同胞们虽然不再充满敌意却本能的避让，红字把这宿命的佩戴者装进了一个强烈的道德孤立圈里。如果这隔离圈以前毫无裨益的话，此时倒是大有好处，让赫斯特能和那船长谈会儿话，不至于被人偷听。何况，赫斯特·普林在公众面前的名声大有改观，即使镇里最守妇道的妇人进行这种谈话，引来的风言风语也不会比她少。

"So, mistress, " said the mariner, "I must bid the steward make ready one more berth than you bargained for! No fear of scurvy or ship-fever, this voyage! What with the ship's surgeon and this other doctor, our only danger will be from drug or pill; more by token, as there is a lot of apothecary's stuff aboard, which I traded for with a Spanish vessel."

“啊，太太，”船长说：“我得让船员在你要求的席位之外，再多安排一个！不用担心在一路上得坏血病或是斑疹伤寒！船上有外科医生和另外这位医生，唯一的危险就是药物了。其实船上还有一批药物呢，是我跟一艘西班牙船交换得来的。”

"What mean you?" inquired Hester, startled more than she permitted to appear. "Have you another passenger?"

“你这是什么意思？”赫斯特问，不禁露出诧异之情。“你有另一位乘客吗？”

"Why, know you not, " cried the shipmaster, "that this physician here — Chillingworth, he calls himself — is minded to try my cabin-fare with you? Ay, ay, you must have known it; for he tells me he is of your party, and a close friend to the gentleman you spoke of — he that is in peril from these sour old Puritan rulers!"

“怎么，你难道不知道吗？”船长叫道，“这里的一位医生——他自称奇林沃斯——打算和你一起尝试我船上的饭菜呢！唉，唉，你肯定知道的。他告诉我你们是一伙的，是你提到的那位先生的密友。你不是说那位先生正受到这些讨厌的老清教统治者的迫害嘛！”

"They know each other well, indeed, " replied Hester, with a mien of calmness, though in the utmost consternation. "They have long dwelt together."

“他们的确彼此很了解。”赫斯特虽已惊愕至极，却故作镇定地答道。“他们住在一起很久了。”

Nothing further passed between the mariner and Hester Prynne. But, at that instant, she beheld old Roger Chillingworth himself, standing in the remotest corner of the market-place and smiling on her; a smile which — across the wide and bustling square, and through all the talk and laughter, and various thoughts, moods, and interests of the crowd — conveyed secret and fearful meaning. 

船长和赫斯特没有再多说什么。不过，就在那一刻，她看到老罗杰·奇林沃斯正站在市场里最偏僻的一个角落，朝她微笑着。那笑容穿过宽阔熙攘的广场，穿过一切欢声笑语，以及人群中种种的念头、情绪和趣味，传递着隐秘而可怕的含义。

# Chapter 22THE PROCESSION

**第二十二章 游行队伍**

Before Hester Prynne could call together her thoughts and consider what was practicable to be done in this new and startling aspect of affairs, the sound of military music was heard approaching along a contiguous street. It denoted the advance of the procession of magistrates and citizens, on its way towards the meeting-house; where, in compliance with a custom thus early established, and ever since observed, the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale was to deliver an Election Sermon. 

赫斯特·普林还没来得及集中思绪，想些切实的措施来应对这新出现的惊人局面，就听到临街传来一阵军乐声，越来越近。这表明官民的游行队伍正朝着议事厅行进。按照早已确立并一直遵循的惯例，丁梅斯代尔牧师先生将在那里进行庆祝选举的布道。

Soon the head of the procession showed itself, with a slow and stately march, turning a corner and making its way across the market-place. First came the music. It comprised a variety of instruments, perhaps imperfectly adapted to one another and played with no great skill; but yet attaining the great object for which the harmony of drum and clarion addresses itself to the multitude — that of imparting a higher and more heroic air to the scene of life that passes before the eye. Little Pearl at first clapped her hands, but then lost, for an instant, the restless agitation that had kept her in a continual effervescence throughout the morning; she gazed silently and seemed to be borne upward, like a floating sea-bird, on the long heaves and swells of sound. But she was brought back to her former mood by the shimmer of the sunshine on the weapons and bright armour of the military company, which followed after the music, and formed the honorary escort of the procession. This body of soldiery — which still sustains a corporate existence nd marches down from past ages with an ancient and honourable fame — was composed of no mercenary materials. Its ranks were filled with gentlemen, who felt the stirrings of martial impulse and sought to establish a kind of College of Arms, where as in an association of Knights Templars, they might learn the science, and, so far as peaceful exercise would teach them, the practices of war. The high estimation then placed upon the military character might be seen in the lofty port of each individual member of the company. Some of them, indeed, by their services in the Low Countries and on other fields of warfare, had fairly won their title to assume the name and pomp of soldiership. The entire array, moreover, clad in burnished steel, and with plumage nodding over their bright morions, had a brilliancy of effect which no modern display can aspire to equal. 

很快，游行队伍的排头出现在视野里，缓慢而庄严地前进着，转过街角，朝市场走来。走在最前面的是乐队。音乐由各式各样的乐器演奏。也许彼此的配合不是很协调，演奏技巧也不高明，但军鼓与铜号的合奏却取得了极好的效果，为眼前的这一生活场景增添了更崇高、更宏伟的气息。小珠儿起先还拍着手，可后来，有一瞬间，使她一早上都处于亢奋状态的情绪突然消失了。她静静地注视着，似乎是被托了起来，像一只海鸟盘旋在汹涌澎湃的波涛声中。乐队之后是充当队列光荣前卫的军人们。他们的武器和甲胄在阳光下闪闪发光，让珠儿又回到了原先的情绪中。这士兵方阵里没有一个雇佣兵，依然保持着一个整体，带着古老而光荣的声名从往昔齐步走来。队列里有不少绅士，他们感到了尚武的冲动，希望建立一种军事学院。在那里，他们能像在圣堂骑士团那样，学习科学，并在和平时期的演习中学会作战。队伍里的每个人都趾高气扬，可见当时军人是备受尊敬的。其中的一些人确实因为在低地国家服役，以及在其他战场上作战，而赢得了军人的头衔和荣耀。而且，整个队列被锃亮的铠甲武装着，上面插着羽毛，上下晃动，营造了一种辉煌的气概，是如今的阅兵所不能与之媲美的。

And yet the men of civil eminence, who came immediately behind the military escort, were better worth a thoughtful observer's eye. Even in outward demeanour, they showed a stamp of majesty that made the warrior's haughty stride look vulgar, if not absurd. It was an age when what we call talent had far less consideration than now, but the massive materials which produce stability and dignity of character a great deal more. The people possessed, by hereditary right, the quality of reverence; which, in their descendants, if it survive at all, exists in smaller proportion, and with a vastly diminished force, in the selection and estimate of public men. The change may be for good or ill, and is partly, perhaps, for both. In that old day, the English settler on these rude shores, having left king, nobles, and all degrees of awful rank behind, while still the faculty and necessity of reverence were strong in him, bestowed it on the white hair and venerable brow of age; on long-tried integrity; on solid wisdom and sad-coloured experience; on endowments of that grave and weighty order which gives the idea of permanence, and comes under the general definition of respectability. These primitive statesmen, therefore — Bradstreet, Endicott, Dudley, Bellingham, and their compeers — who were elevated to power by the early choice of the people, seem to have been not often brilliant, but distinguished by a ponderous sobriety rather than activity of intellect. They had fortitude and self-reliance, and, in time of difficulty or peril, stood up for the welfare of the state like a line of cliffs against a tempestuous tide. The traits of character here indicated were well represented in the square cast of countenance and large physical development of the new colonial magistrates. So far as a demeanour of natural authority was concerned, the mother country need not have been ashamed to see these foremost men of an actual democracy adopted into the House of Peers, or made the Privy Council of the sovereign. 

紧随着卫队的文职官员，有头脑的旁观者认为那些人更值得关注。即便是从外在举止看，他们也颇具庄严之气。相比之下，那些士兵傲慢的步伐即使不是荒谬，也颇显庸俗了些。在那个年代，天才远没像今天这样受到重视，而那些能营造稳定、促成人格尊严的多种因素，却大受青睐。人们拥有世袭而来的尊贵；这在他们的后继者身上，即使侥幸存在，比例也要小得多，而且由于官员的选拔和评估，他们的势力也要大大减少。这变化也许是好事，也许是坏事，也许好坏兼有。昔日，到这荒蛮海滨定居的英国移民，他们虽然抛弃了王公贵族和种种令人生畏的头衔，内心却存留着强烈的尊贵本色和需求，他们将其加诸于苍苍的白发和年迈的额头。加诸于久经考验的正直，加诸于坚实的智慧和富于阴郁色彩的经历，加诸于严肃沉重、给人以永恒之感、以得体为名义的秩序。因此早年这些由人民推举而当政的政治家——布拉德斯特里特、恩迪科特、达德利、贝林厄姆以及他们的同辈——这些人往往并没有多么英明，他们受人尊敬似乎并不是因为他们有什么睿智之举，而是因为他们的沉稳和节制。他们坚定而自信，就像直面怒涛的悬崖之边，能在危难之时为了国家利益挺身而出。这里提及的品格特征，充分体现在这些新殖民地长官的四方脸和大块头上。就这些天生当权者的举止而言，即使英格兰看到民主的先驱们进入贵族院，或是委以枢密院顾问之要职，它也不用感到羞耻。

Next in order to the magistrates came the young and eminently distinguished divine, from whose lips the religious discourse of the anniversary was expected. His was the profession, at that era, in which intellectual ability displayed itself far more than in political life; for — leaving a higher motive out of the question — it offered inducements powerful enough, in the almost worshipping respect of the community, to win the most aspiring ambition into its service. Even political power — as in the case of Increase Mather — was within the grasp of a successful priest. 

官员后面是那位声名显赫的年轻牧师，人们期待着从他的嘴里听到本次庆祝日的宗教演说。在那个年代，牧师一职所显露的智能，远多于政治生活中所能显露的。因为撇开更高的动机不谈，在引起居民近乎崇拜的尊敬这点上，它就足以有力地激励并赢得最有抱负之人投身其间。甚至政治权力也会落入成功的牧师手中，英克里斯·马瑟就是一例。

It was the observation of those who beheld him now, that never, since Mr. Dimmesdale first set his foot on the New England shore, had he exhibited such energy as was seen in the gait and air with which he kept his pace in the procession. There was no feebleness of step, as at other times; his frame was not bent; nor did his hand rest ominously upon his heart. Yet, if the clergyman were rightly viewed, his strength seemed not of the body. It might be spiritual, and imparted to him by angelic ministrations. It might be the exhilaration of that potent cordial which is distilled only in the furnace glow of earnest and long-continued thought. Or, perchance, his sensitive temperament was invigorated by the loud and piercing music that swelled heavenward, and uplifted him on its ascending wave. Nevertheless, so abstracted was his look, it might be questioned whether Mr. Dimmesdale even heard the music. There was his body, moving onward, and with an unaccustomed force. But where was his mind? Far and deep in its own region, busying itself, with preternatural activity, to marshal a procession of stately thoughts that were soon to issue thence; and so he saw nothing, heard nothing, knew nothing, of what was around him; but the spiritual element took up the feeble frame, and carried it along, unconscious of the burden, and converting it to spirit like himself. Men of uncommon intellect, who have grown morbid, possess this occasional power of mighty effort, into which they throw the life of many days, and then are lifeless for as many more. 

这时，人们注意到，他步态矫健、精神抖擞地走在队伍之中。丁梅斯代尔先生自踏上新英格兰的土地以来，从未像今天这样精力充沛。他的步伐没有以往那么虚弱，不再弓着背了，也不再病态地把手捂在心口了。不过，要是细细观察牧师，就能发现，他的精力似乎不是源自身体。那精力也许源自精神，由天使通过宗教仪式赋予他。那是潜在热情的兴奋表现——这热情只有在长期不断的诚挚思想的熔炉中，才能被蒸馏出来。或者，也许是他敏感的气质被升腾着的响亮刺耳的音乐所鼓舞，把他托起在音乐之浪中。不过，他的表情是如此迷离，人们甚至会怀疑，丁梅斯代尔先生是否听到了这乐声。他的身体在一种异常的力量推动下，向前行进。但他的心灵在哪里呢？他远远地深藏在自己的领域里，忙于超自然的活动，以清理不久将说出的一系列庄重的思想。因此，他什么都看不到，听不到，对周身的事情一无所知。那精神支撑起他虚弱的身体，带他前行，不但对重荷无所察觉，还把它转换成像自身一样的精神。那些病态却具非凡智慧的人，通过巨大的努力获得这偶然的能力，把多天的生命凝聚于一时，而在接下来的许多日子里却是了无生气。

Hester Prynne, gazing steadfastly at the clergyman, felt a dreary influence come over her, but wherefore or whence she knew not; unless that he seemed so remote from her own sphere, and utterly beyond her reach. One glance of recognition, she had imagined, must needs pass between them. She thought of the dim forest, with its little dell of solitude, and love, and anguish, and the mossy tree-trunk, where, sitting hand in hand, they had mingled their sad and passionate talk with the melancholy murmur of the brook. How deeply had they known each other then! And was this the man? She hardly knew him now! He, moving proudly past, enveloped, as it were, in the rich music, with the procession of majestic and venerable fathers; he, so unattainable in his worldly position, and still more so in that far vista of his unsympathising thoughts, through which she now beheld him! Her spirit sank with the idea that all must have been a delusion, and that, vividly as she had dreamed it, there could be no real bond betwixt the clergyman and herself. And thus much of woman was there in Hester, that she could scarcely forgive him — least of all now, when the heavy footstep of their approaching Fate might be heard, nearer, nearer, nearer! — for being able so completely to withdraw himself from their mutual world; while she groped darkly, and stretched forth her cold hands, and found him not. 

赫斯特·普林紧紧地注视着牧师，感到一股阴沉的势力袭遍全身，却不知它为何而来，从何而来。她只觉得他离自己的天地好远，全然不可触及。她曾想象过，他俩之间一定会交换一次彼此心照的眼色。她想起了那昏暗的树林，那寂静的山谷，还有那爱情，和那极度的痛苦。在长满青苔的树干上，他们携手而坐，把二人忧愁而热切的交谈融于小溪忧郁的低语之中。当时，他们是多么息息相通啊！眼前的这人是他吗？她几乎不认识他了！他在低沉的乐声中，随着威严可敬的神父们高傲地走了过去。他在尘世的地位如此高不可攀，而她现在察觉到，他那深不可测的思想更是可望而不可即！她想到，虽然自己梦得如此真切，但一切都是幻觉，她和牧师之间没有任何实际的联系，精神便消沉了下去。作为女人，赫斯特简直难以原谅他——特别是此刻，他俩的命运迈着沉重的脚步，声音越来越近！而他竟可以从两人的共同世界里全然而退。她在黑暗中摸索，伸开冰冷的双臂，却遍寻不到他。

Pearl either saw and responded to her mother's feelings, or herself felt the remoteness and intangibility that had fallen around the minister. While the procession passed, the child was uneasy, fluttering up and down like a bird on the point of taking flight. When the whole had gone by, she looked up into Hester's face. 

珠儿也许是察觉了母亲的感受，也许是感应到了，要不就是她自己也觉得牧师已笼罩在遥不可及之中。当游行队伍经过时，珠儿像只跃跃欲飞的鸟儿，不安地上下扑腾。当整个队伍经过后，她抬头望着赫斯特的脸。

"Mother, " said she, "was that the same minister that kissed me by the brook?"

“妈妈，”她说，“那个就是在溪边亲了我的牧师吗？”

"Hold thy peace, dear little Pearl!" whispered her mother. "We must not always talk in the market-place of what happens to us in the forest. "

“别作声，亲爱的小珠儿！”母亲低语道。“我们可不能老在市场上讲我们在树林里遇到的事情。”

"I could not be sure that it was he; so strange he looked, " continued the child. "Else I would have run to him, and bid him kiss me now, before all the people; even as he did yonder among the dark old trees. What would the minister have said, mother? Would he have clapped his hand over his heart, and scowled on me, and bid me begone?"

“我弄不准那是不是他，他看上去好奇怪。”孩子接着说。“不然我就跑过去，让他当着大家的面亲我了——就像他在那黑漆漆的老树林里做的一样。牧师会说些什么呢，妈妈？他会把手捂在心口，对我瞪眼睛，让我走开吗？”

"What should he say, Pearl?" answered Hester, "save that it was no time to kiss, and that kisses are not to be given in the market-place? Well for thee, foolish child, that thou didst not speak to him!"

“他能说些什么呢，珠儿？”赫斯特问道，“除了说，这不是亲你的时候，在市场上是不能亲你的？总算还好，傻孩子，你没有和他讲话！”

Another shade of the same sentiment in reference to Mr. Dimmesdale was expressed by a person whose eccentricities — or insanity, as we should term it — led her to do what few of the townspeople would have ventured on; to begin a conversation with the wearer of the scarlet letter, in public. It was Mistress Hibbins, who, arrayed in great magnificence, with a triple ruff, a broidered stomacher, a gown of rich velvet, and a gold-headed cane, had come forth to see the procession. As this ancient lady had the renown (which subsequently cost her no less a price than her life) of being a principal actor in all the works of necromancy that were continually going forward, the crowd gave way before her, and seemed to fear the touch of her garment, as if it carried the plague among its gorgeous folds. Seen in conjunction with Hester Prynne — kindly as so many now felt towards the latter — the dread inspired by Mistress Hibbins was doubled, and caused a general movement from that part of the market-place in which the two women stood. 

对于丁梅斯代尔牧师，还有一个人也表达了相同的感受，她竟然荒唐到——或者应该说是疯狂到——做了件很少镇里人敢做的事情：她在大庭广众之下和那戴着红字的人讲起话来。那人是西宾斯太太。她套着三层皱领，罩着绣花胸衣，穿着华丽的绒袍，握着根金头手杖，打扮得一派富贵，出来观看游行。这老妇人为人所知，是因为她在那些持续流行的巫术中担任主角，最终起码让她付出了生命的代价。人群为她让出一条路，似乎唯恐触到她的衣袍，仿佛那华丽的衣褶里携带着瘟疫。看到西宾斯太太和赫斯特在一起——尽管人们现在对后者怀有好感，但人们对西宾斯太太的惧怕还是倍增了——大家纷纷挪离两人站的地方。

"Now, what mortal imagination could conceive it!" whispered the old lady confidentially to Hester. "Yonder divine man! That saint on earth, as the people uphold him to be, and as — I must needs say — he really looks! Who, now, that saw him pass in the procession, would think how little while it is since he went forth out of his study — chewing a Hebrew text of Scripture in his mouth, I warrant — to take an airing in the forest! Aha! We know what that means, Hester Prynne! But, truly, forsooth, I find it hard to believe him the same man. Many a church-member saw I, walking behind the music, that has danced in the same measure with me, when Somebody was fiddler, and, it might be, an Indian powwow or a Lapland wizard changing hands with us! That is but a trifle, when a woman knows the world. But this minister! Couldst thou surely tell, Hester, whether he was the same man that encountered thee on the forest-path?"

“哎，凡夫俗子能想出些什么呢！”老太婆悄悄地对赫斯特说。“瞧那神圣的人！人们觉得他是人间圣者，我得说，他看上去还真像那么回事！现在看着他行进在队伍里的人，谁能想到在不久前，他还从书房里出来，去树林里逍遥呢！——我担保，当时他嘴里还念着希伯来文的《圣经》呢。啊哈！我们清楚那是什么意思，赫斯特·普林！不过，说实话，我还是难以相信他就是那同一个人。我看见这么多教民跟在乐队后面游行——他们都曾随着我踏着同样的舞步，那时还有人拉着小提琴，也许还有一个印第安人祭司或拉普兰法师同我们牵着手呢！当女人看透了这世界，这不过是小事一桩。但这个人是牧师啊！赫斯特，你能肯定地说，他就是和你相遇在林间小道上的那个人吗？”

"Madam, I know not of what you speak, " answered Hester Prynne, feeling Mistress Hibbins to be of infirm mind; yet strangely startled and awe-stricken by the confidence with which she affirmed a personal connection between so many persons (herself among them) and the Evil One. "It is not for me to talk lightly of a learned and pious minister of the Word, like the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale!"

“夫人，我不明白你在说什么，”赫斯特·普林回答道。她觉得西宾斯太太是有点老糊涂了。不过，老太婆如此自信地肯定，这么多人（包括她自己在内）和那邪恶的家伙有私人联系。对此，她异常吃惊，并且吓得要命。“我可没有资格随便谈论像丁梅斯代尔牧师先生那样的牧师，他学识渊博又虔信《圣经》!"

"Fie, woman, fie!" cried the old lady, shaking her finger at Hester. "Dost thou think I have been to the forest so many times, and have yet no skill to judge who else has been there? Yea; though no leaf of the wild garlands, which they wore while they danced, be left in their hair! I know thee, Hester; for I behold the token. We may all see it in the sunshine; and it glows like a red flame in the dark. Thou wearest it openly; so there need be no question about that. But this minister! Let me tell thee, in thine ear! When the Black Man sees one of his own servants, signed and sealed, so shy of owning to the bond as is the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale, he hath a way of ordering matters so that the mark shall be disclosed in open daylight to the eyes of all the world! What is it that the minister seeks to hide, with his hand always over his heart? Ha, Hester Prynne!"

“呸，女人，呸！”老太婆冲赫斯特摇着一个指头叫道。“你以为我去树林那么多次，还没有本事判断还有谁去过那儿？虽然他们跳舞时戴的花环没有在头发上留下叶子，但我当然知道！我知道你，赫斯特，因为我看到了那个标记。我们都能在阳光下看到它，而在黑夜里，它就如红色之焰般闪光。你公开地戴着它，因此绝不会弄错的。可是这位牧师！让我在你耳朵边讲给你听吧！当黑男人看到自己的一个仆人，叹着气，盖着章，像丁梅斯代尔牧师先生那样羞于承认有这么个协议时，他自有一套办法让标记暴露在光天化日之下、大庭广众之前！牧师用手捂着心口，到底要隐藏什么呢？哈，赫斯特·普林！”

"What is it, good Mistress Hibbins?" eagerly asked little Pearl. "Hast thou seen it?"

“是什么呢，好心的西宾斯太太？”小珠儿急切地问道。“你见过吗？”

"No matter, darling!" responded Mistress Hibbins, making Pearl a profound reverence. "Thou thyself wilt see it, one time or another. They say, child, thou art of the lineage of the Prince of the Air! Wilt thou ride with me, some fine night, to see thy father? Then thou shalt know wherefore the minister keeps his hand over his heart!"

“别管它，可爱的小东西！”西宾斯太太对珠儿毕恭毕敬地说。“总有一天你会看到的。孩子，他们说你是‘空中王子’的后代呢！你要不要在某个晚上，和我一起腾云驾雾，去见见你的爸爸呀？到时你就知道牧师为什么用手捂着心口了！”

Laughing so shrilly that all the market-place could hear her, the weird old gentlewoman took her departure. 

这古怪的老太太尖声大笑着走开了，弄得整个市场都能听到。

By this time the preliminary prayer had been offered in the meeting-house, and the accents of the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale were heard commencing his discourse. An irresistible feeling kept Hester near the spot. As the sacred edifice was too much thronged to admit another auditor, she took up her position close beside the scaffold of the pillory. It was in sufficient proximity to bring the whole sermon to her ears, in the shape of an indistinct, but varied, murmur and flow of the minister's very peculiar voice. 

此时，议事厅里已经做完了场前祈祷，人们可以听到丁梅斯代尔牧师先生开始布道了。一种不可抗拒的心情促使赫斯特走近。这神圣的大楼被挤得密不透风，于是她在靠近刑台的地方占了个位置。这地方足可以听到全部的讲道。牧师的声音虽并不响亮，却富有特色、抑扬顿挫，如流水的低吟。

This vocal organ was in itself a rich endowment; insomuch that a listener, comprehending nothing of the language in which the preacher spoke, might still have been swayed to and fro by the mere tone and cadence. Like all other music, it breathed passion and pathos, and emotions high or tender, in a tongue native to the human heart, wherever educated. Muffled as the sound was by its passage through the church walls, Hester Prynne listened with such intentness, and sympathised so intimately, that the sermon had throughout a meaning for her, entirely apart from its indistinguishable words. These, perhaps, if more distinctly heard, might have been only a grosser medium, and have clogged the spiritual sense. Now she caught the low undertone, as of the wind sinking down to repose itself; then ascended with it, as it rose through progressive gradations of sweetness and power, until its volume seemed to envelop her with an atmosphere of awe and solemn grandeur. And yet, majestic as the voice sometimes became, there was forever in it an essential character of plaintiveness. A loud or low expression of anguish — the whisper, or the shriek, as it might be conceived, of suffering humanity, that touched a sensibility in every bosom! At times this deep strain of pathos was all that could be heard, and scarcely heard, sighing amid a desolate silence. But even when the minister's voice grew high and commanding — when it gushed irrepressibly upward — when it assumed its utmost breadth and power, so overfilling the church as to burst its way through the solid walls and diffuse itself in the open air — still, if the auditor listened intently, and for the purpose, he could detect the same cry of pain. What was it? The complaint of a human heart, sorrow-laden, perchance guilty, telling its secret, whether of guilt or sorrow, to the great heart of mankind; beseeching its sympathy or forgiveness — at every moment — in each accent — and never in vain! It was this profound and continual undertone that gave the clergyman his most appropriate power. 

人能发声，这本身就是种珍贵的天赋，听众即便对布道者所说一概不知，却依旧能仅因语气和抑扬顿挫的音调而心驰神往。和其他音乐一样，它传达着激情和悲怆，传达着高昂抑或柔和的情绪。不管你受教于何方，都会感到那是人类的心灵母语。虽然声音因穿过教堂的墙壁而显得低沉，但赫斯特·普林听得那么聚精会神，和布道产生了如此贴近的共鸣。在难辨的字面意义之外，这讲道对她有另一番完整的意义。如果这些话语能被听得更清楚些，也许就只成了一种粗俗的工具，反倒阻碍了它精神的含义。如今她听着低低的声音，仿佛是减弱的风势要歇口气。之后，随着那甜美而充满力量的声音逐渐上扬，她的精神也为之振奋起来，直到那声音最后用敬畏、庄严的气氛将她包裹起来。然而，不论这声音变得多么威严，其间永远透着哀伤的本色。这些听上去或高或低的、表达极度痛苦和苦难人生的低语或叫喊，触动了每个人的心扉！有时，这低沉而悲怆的旋律成为你听到的所有声音，有时——这种情况很少——你也能隐约地听到寂静中的哀叹。但即便牧师的声音变得高亢而威严，当那声音不可抑制地直冲云霄，当那声音最为宽厚有力，充斥着教堂，几乎要破壁而出，融于露天之中时，旁听者要是洗耳恭听，还是能分辨出同样的痛苦呼号。那是什么呢？是一颗满负忧伤，也许也充满着罪恶感的心灵在哀怨，向人类伟大的胸怀诉说心中的秘密。不论是愧疚或是悲伤，每时每刻，它的每个声音都在请求同情或宽恕，从来都不会徒劳！这深邃持续的语调给了牧师最恰到好处的力量。

During all this time, Hester stood, statue-like, at the foot of the scaffold. If the minister's voice had not kept her there, there would nevertheless have been an inevitable magnetism in that spot, whence she dated the first hour of her life of ignominy. There was a sense within her — to ill-defined to be made a thought, but weighing heavily on her mind — that her whole orb of life, both before and after, was connected with this spot, as with the one point that gave it unity. 

这期间，赫斯特像一尊雕像般，一直站在刑台脚下。如果不是牧师的声音把她吸引在那里的话，那地方必然有一种磁力——她在那里经受了耻辱人生的第一个小时。她有种感觉——这感觉因其隐约而不能被称为思想，却重重地压在她脑海里——她之前之后的整个人生轨迹都与这地方联系在一起了，似乎是这一点才把她的生活连为一体。

Little Pearl, meanwhile, had quitted her mother's side, and was playing at her own will about the market-place. She made the sombre crowd cheerful by her erratic and glistening ray; even as a bird of bright plumage illuminates a whole tree of dusty foliage by darting to and fro, half seen and half concealed amid the twilight of the clustering leaves. She had an undulating, but, oftentimes, a sharp and irregular movement. It indicated the restless vivacity of her spirit, which today was doubly indefatigable in its tiptoe dance, because it was played upon and vibrated with her mother's disquietude. Whenever Pearl saw anything to excite her ever-active and wandering curiosity, she flew thitherward, and, as we might say, seized upon that man or thing as her own property, so far as she desired it; but without yielding the minutest degree of control over her motions in requital. The Puritans looked on, and, if they smiled, were none the less inclined to pronounce the child a demon offspring, from the indescribable charm of beauty and eccentricity that shone through her little figure, and sparkled with its activity. She ran and looked the wild Indian in the face; and he grew conscious of a nature wilder than his own. 

与此同时，小珠儿已经离开了她母亲身边，顾自在市场上到处玩开了。她如同反复无常闪动的光线，令严肃的人群变得活跃起来。她甚至像一只羽毛光鲜夺目的小鸟，飞来飞去，在叶簇幽暗的光影里若隐若现，照亮了整棵被灰尘覆盖的树。她行踪飘忽，时常会作出突然而意外的动作。这表明了她那活泼好动的精神劲儿。今天，由于受她母亲不安之情的拨弄和挑动，那足尖就舞跳得愈发不知疲倦了。珠儿的好奇心一直很活跃，无论何时，只要见到任何能激起她好奇心的事物，可以说只要她愿意，她就会飞似地过去抓住那个人或东西，当作自己的财产，绝不稍稍控制下自己的行动来作为回报。清教徒们看着珠儿。他们即便露出微笑，也没少把这孩子看作魔鬼的后代。因为她那从小小的身躯发散着难以名状的魅力，美丽而古怪，随着她的动作而闪烁。她跑过去，盯着那粗野的印第安人的脸；那人便意识到竟有比自己更加天性狂野的人。

Thence, with native audacity, but still with a reserve as characteristic, she flew into the midst of a group of mariners, the swarthy-cheeked wild men of the ocean, as the Indians were of the land; and they gazed wonderingly and admiringly at Pearl, as if a flake on the sea-foam had taken the shape of a little maid, and were gifted with a soul of the sea-fire that flashes beneath the prow in the night-time. 

后来，由于天生放肆、却依旧有些拘谨的性格，她又飞进一群水手中间。那群面颊黝黑的海上野蛮人，就好似陆地上的印第安人。他们惊羡地瞅着珠儿，好像她是海的泡沫化身成的少女，被赋予海火的灵魂，在夜晚的船首下闪烁。

One of these seafaring men — the shipmaster, indeed, who had spoken to Hester Prynne — was so smitten with Pearl's aspect, that he attempted to lay hands upon her, with purpose to snatch a kiss. Finding it as impossible to touch her as to catch a humming-bird in the air, he took from his hat the gold chain that was twisted about it, and threw it to the child. Pearl immediately twined it around her neck and waist, with such happy skill that, once seen there, it became a part of her, and it was difficult to imagine her without it. 

其中的一个水手——就是那个同赫斯特·普林谈过话的船长——被珠儿的模样深深吸引，便试图把手放在她头上，打算亲亲她。他发现，要碰到她简直就像抓一只在空中飞鸣而过的小鸟一样，根本不可能。于是他就把围在帽子上的金链拿下来，扔给了孩子。珠儿立马巧妙地把金链围在了脖子上和腰间。一眼看去，那金链就像是她的一部分，让人难以想象她没有金链的样子。

"Thy mother is yonder woman with the scarlet letter, " said the seaman. "Wilt thou carry her a message from me?"

“你妈妈就是那边戴着红字的女人吗？”船长问。“你替我捎个口信好吗？”

"If the message pleases me, I will, " answered Pearl. 

“要是那口信讨我喜欢，我就捎。”珠儿回答。

"Then tell her, " rejoined he, "that I spake again with the black-a-visaged hump-shouldered old doctor, and he engages to bring his friend, the gentleman she wots of, aboard with him. So let thy mother take no thought, save for herself and thee. Wilt thou tell her this, thou witch-baby?"

“那就告诉她，”他接着说，“我又和那黑脸驼背的老医生讲过了，他保证带着他的朋友上船——就是你妈妈提到过的那位。所以你妈妈除了照顾好你和她自己，就不用操心别的了。你能告诉她吗，你这小妖精？”

"Mistress Hibbins says my father is the Prince of the Air!" cried Pearl, with a naughty smile. "If thou callest me that ill name, I shall tell him of thee, and he will chase thy ship with a tempest!"

“西宾斯太太说我爸爸是空中王子！”珠儿一边调皮地笑着，一边叫道。“你要是再叫得那么难听，我就告诉他，他会用暴风雨追你的船！”

Pursuing a zigzag course across the market-place, the child returned to her mother and communicated what the mariner had said. Hester's strong, calm, steadfastly enduring spirit almost sank, at last, on beholding this dark and grim countenance of an inevitable doom, which — at the moment when a passage seemed to open for the minister and herself out of their labyrinth of misery — showed itself, with an unrelenting smile, right in the midst of their path. 

这孩子沿着弯弯曲曲的路线穿过市场，回到母亲身边，转告了船长的话。看到这不可避免的命运其阴森面目后，赫斯特那坚强、镇定、坚韧不拔的精神，终于崩溃了。就在一条通路似乎为她和牧师而敞开，要领他们走出悲惨的迷宫时，这带着毫不留情的冷笑的面孔，恰恰挡在他们的路中间。

With her mind harassed by the terrible perplexity in which the shipmaster's intelligence involved her, she was also subjected to another trial. There were many people present from the country round about who had often heard of the scarlet letter, and to whom it had been made terrific by a hundred false or exaggerated rumours, but who had never beheld it with their own bodily eyes. These, after exhausting other modes of amusement, now thronged about Hester Prynne with rude and boorish intrusiveness. Unscrupulous as it was, however, it could not bring them nearer than a circuit of several yards. At that distance they accordingly stood, fixed there by the centrifugal force of the repugnance which the mystic symbol inspired. The whole gang of sailors, likewise, observing the press of spectators, and learning the purport of the scarlet letter, came and thrust their sunburnt and desperado-looking faces into the ring. Even the Indians were affected by a sort of cold shadow of the white man's curiosity, and, gliding through the crowd, fastened their snake-like black eyes on Hester's bosom, conceiving, perhaps, that the wearer of this brilliantly embroidered badge must needs be a personage of high dignity among her people. Lastly, the inhabitants of the town (their own interest in this worn-out subject languidly reviving itself, by sympathy with what they saw others feel) lounged idly to the same quarter, and tormented Hester Prynne, perhaps more than all the rest, with their cool, well-acquainted gaze at her familiar shame. Hester saw and recognised the self-same faces of that group of matrons, who had awaited her forthcoming from the prison-door, seven years ago; all save one, the youngest and only compassionate among them, whose burial-robe she had since made. At the final hour, when she was so soon to fling aside the burning letter, it had strangely become the centre of more remark and excitement, and was thus made to sear her breast more painfully than at any time since the first day she put it on. 

船长捎来的这条消息，带给了她可怕的困惑，扰得她心神不宁。可是，她还要面对另一个考验。市场里很多从乡下来的人经常听说红字的事，却从来没有亲眼目睹过。对他们来说，数以百计的误传和夸张的谣言，使红字变得十分可怕。这些人看腻了其他的娱乐，此刻便粗鲁无礼地挤在赫斯特·普林身边。他们肆无忌惮地挤过来，却还是停在六七米远的圈子外。他们就这么远地站着，被那神秘之符所激起的反感离心力钉在那里。那帮水手也注意到了围观的人，弄明白了那红字的涵义，也凑过去，把被太阳晒得黝黑的、亡命徒般的面孔伸进圈子里。连印第安人也受到了白人好奇心的无声影响，穿过人群，眯起蛇一般的黑眼睛，盯着赫斯特的胸前。他们或许以为，那戴着耀眼夺目的丝绣徽记的人，准是这群人中德高望重之辈。最后，镇上的居民（看到别人的反应，他们也无精打采地恢复了对这个陈旧话题的兴趣）懒散地挪到这一角落，用他们冰冷惯见的目光注视着赫斯特·普林熟悉的耻辱标记，这或许比别人对她的折磨更甚。赫斯特看到并认出了跟七年前相同的那几张脸，她们是等她走出监狱大门的那群女看守。只是少了一人，就是她们中最年轻，且是唯一有同情心的人；赫斯特后来给她做了葬服。在她即将甩掉这灼人红字的最后时刻，它居然莫名其妙地成为了更能引起谈资和兴奋的中心。自戴上红字以来，她还从未如此痛苦地感到，它在她的胸膛上灼烧。

While Hester stood in that magic circle of ignominy where the cunning cruelty of her sentence seemed to have fixed her forever, the admirable preacher was looking down from the sacred pulpit upon an audience whose very inmost spirits had yielded to his control. The sainted minister in the church! The woman of the scarlet letter in the market-place! What imagination would have been irreverent enough to surmise that the same scorching stigma was on them both! 

赫斯特站在那耻辱的魔力圈里，那狡诈而残忍的判决似乎将她永远钉在那里。与此同时，可敬的布道者在神圣的祭坛上俯视着他的听众，他们最内在的精神臣服于他的掌握。这教堂里神圣的牧师啊！这市场里戴着红字的女人啊！怎样不敬的想象才能猜到，他们有着同一个灼热的污名啊！

# Chapter 23THE REVELATION OF THE SCARLET LETTER

**第二十三章 红字的显露**

The eloquent voice, on which the souls of the listening audience had been borne aloft as on the swelling waves of the sea, at length came to a pause. There was a momentary silence, profound as what should follow the utterance of oracles. Then ensued a murmur and half-hushed tumult; as if the auditors, released from the high spell that had transported them into the region of another's mind, were returning into themselves, with all their awe and wonder still heavy on them. In a moment more, the crowd began to gush forth from the doors of the church. Now that there was an end, they needed other breath, more fit to support the gross and earthly life into which they relapsed, than that atmosphere which the preacher had converted into words of flame and had burdened with the rich fragrance of his thought. 

那雄辩的话音，如汹涌的海浪般，托起了听众的灵魂。终于，那话音停了下来。那瞬时的寂静，如同宣告了神谕后一般深沉。随后是一阵低语和小声的骚动声，仿佛听众从将他们带到另一种思想境界的高级咒语中解脱了出来，回归自身，可那敬畏惊奇之感依然沉重于心。又过了一会儿，人群开始从教堂的大门蜂拥而出。如今布道已经结束，人们需要换换气。因为牧师的语言如火焰一般燃烧着，散发出自身思想之馥郁芬芳，与此种氛围相比，另一种气息更适于凡夫俗子的生活。

In the open air their rapture broke into speech. The street and the market-place absolutely babbled, from side to side, with applauses of the minister. His hearers could not rest until they had told one another of what each knew better than he could tell or hear. According to their united testimony, never had man spoken in so wise, so high, and so holy a spirit, as he that spake this day; nor had inspiration ever breathed through mortal lips more evidently than it did through his. Its influence could be seen, as it were, descending upon him, and possessing him, and continually lifting him out of the written discourse that lay before him, and filling him with ideas that must have been as marvellous to himself as to his audience. His subject, it appeared, had been the relation between the Deity and the communities of mankind, with a special reference to the New England which they were here planting in the wilderness. And, as he drew towards the close, a spirit as of prophecy had come upon him, constraining him to its purpose as mightily as the old prophets of Israel were constrained; only with this difference, that, whereas the Jewish seers had denounced judgments and ruin on their country, it was his mission to foretell a high and glorious destiny for the newly gathered people of the Lord. But, throughout it all, and through the whole discourse, there had been a certain deep, sad undertone of pathos, which could not be interpreted otherwise than as the natural regret of one soon to pass away. Yes; their minister whom they so loved — and who so loved them all, that he could not depart heavenward without a sigh — had the foreboding of untimely death upon him, and would soon leave them in their tears! This idea of his transitory stay on earth gave the last emphasis to the effect which the preacher had produced; it was as if an angel, in his passage to the skies, had shaken his bright wings over the people for an instant — at once a shadow and a splendour — and had shed down a shower of golden truths upon them. 

在户外，他们的狂喜迸发成语言。街道上，市场中，到处是对牧师的溢美之词。他的听众们彼此诉说着所知道的一切，直到说尽听够为止。他们一致证实，没有人像他今天这样睿智、崇高、而富有神圣气息，也没有人能说出如此颇具启发性的话语。可以说，他的影响力源于他，也左右着他，不断地把他从面前的讲稿里提升出来，用对他本人和对听众来说都妙不可言的思想充实他。他讲的主题是神和人类社会的关系，其中特别谈到了他们垦荒播种的新英格兰。当他的布道接近尾声时，一种预言之灵降临于他，如同当时约束以色列老预言家那样，强有力地迫使他就范。唯一不同的是，犹太预言家宣告了国家的天罚和灭亡，而他的使命是预言，这些新集结的上帝臣民将有一个崇高而光荣的命运。但整个发言有一种深沉悲哀的基调，人们只能理解为是一个即将离去之人自然的忏悔。是的，他们如此爱戴的牧师，同样爱着他们，他不能不叹一声气就离开他们，飞往天国啊！他预感死亡将不合时宜地降临，自己将在他们的眼泪中离去！一想到他弥留世上的时间已经不多，牧师那番布道产生的效果就更加明显了。仿佛天使在去往天国的路上，在人们头顶扇动了一下明亮的翅膀，瞬时光影交替，洒下一阵黄金般的真理。

Thus, there had come to the Reverend Mr. Dimmesdale — as to most men, in their various spheres, though seldom recognised until they see it far behind them — an epoch of life more brilliant and full of triumph than any previous one, or than any which could hereafter be. He stood, at this moment, on the very proudest eminence of superiority, to which the gifts of intellect, rich lore, prevailing eloquence, and a reputation of whitest sanctity, could exalt a clergyman in New England's earliest days, when the professional character was of itself a lofty pedestal. Such was the position which the minister occupied, as he bowed his head forward on the cushions of the pulpit, at the close of his Election Sermon. Meanwhile Hester Prynne was standing beside the scaffold of the pillory, with the scarlet letter still burning her breast! 

于是丁梅斯代尔牧师先生来到了人生中最辉煌最充满胜利的时期——大多数人在不同领域也有这样的时期，但往往时过境迁了才意识到。在新英格兰早期，牧师这个职业本来就是养尊处优的。他那智慧之禀赋，学识之深厚，口才之超凡，以及最圣洁的名声，更是在此刻提升了他，让他站到最让人引以为傲的卓越之巅。当牧师结束了他在庆祝日的布道，俯首于讲坛上的靠垫时，所处的正是这样的高位。与此同时，赫斯特正站在刑台边上，那红字在她胸前燃烧！

Now was heard again the clangour of the music, and the measured tramp of the military escort, issuing from the church-door. The procession was to be marshalled thence to the town-hall, where a solemn banquet would complete the ceremonies of the day. 

音乐声再次响起，卫队那缓慢而有节奏的步伐，从教堂大门传来。游行队伍将从那里行进至镇议事厅——那里将举行庄重的宴会，来结束这一天的庆典。

Once more, therefore, the train of venerable and majestic fathers was seen moving through a broad pathway of the people, who drew back reverently on either side as the Governor and magistrates, the old and wise men, the holy ministers, and all that were eminent and renowned, advanced into the midst of them. When they were fairly in the market-place, their presence was greeted by a shout. This — though doubtless it might acquire additional force and volume from the childlike loyalty which the age awarded to its rulers — was felt to be an irrepressible outburst of enthusiasm kindled in the auditors by that high strain of eloquence which was yet reverberating in their ears. Each felt the impulse in himself, and, in the same breath, caught it from his neighbour. Within the church, it had hardly been kept down; beneath the sky, it pealed upward to the zenith. There were human beings enough, and enough of highly wrought and symphonious feeling, to produce that more impressive sound than the organ tones of the blast, or the thunder, or the roar of the sea; even that mighty swell of many voices, blended into one great voice by the universal impulse which makes likewise one vast heart out of the many. Never, from the soil of New England, had gone up such a shout! Never, on New England soil, had stood the man so honoured by his mortal brethren as the preacher! 

于是，那一列可敬威严的人士再一次行进在宽阔的道路上，路边是围观的群众。当总督、长官、贤明的长者、神圣的牧师，以及所有德高望重的人行进在他们中间时，人们满腹敬畏地向后退避。这支队伍出现在市场上时，人群欢呼着向他们致意。无疑，那因孩子般的忠贞而额外增添的声势，是时代给予统治者的赏赐，却也被认为是听众热情不可抑制的爆发——那热情被雄辩的口才所激起，依旧回荡在人们耳边。每个人不但自己感到了冲动，也在旁边人身上感到了类似的冲动。在教堂里，这种冲动就已难以压制；在天空之下，它响彻云霄。那人群，那高度契合的情感，足以制造令人印象深刻的声响，甚于那爆破之音、雷声轰鸣、海浪呼啸。人们甚至在共同的冲动之下，带着同一颗心，众声融合成一声，发出巨响。新英格兰的土地上，从未爆发过这样的欢呼！在新英格兰的土地上，从未有人像这位布道者般，受世间的同胞如此的敬重！

How fared it with him then? Were there not the brilliant particles of a halo in the air about his head! So etherealised by spirit as he was, and so apotheosised by worshipping admirers, did his footsteps, in the procession, really tread upon the dust of earth? 

那他后来怎么样了呢？他头顶不是有灿烂的光环吗！他被神灵感化地如此脱俗，又被崇拜者如此地神化，那么，他游行时的脚步真地踏在凡尘之上了吗？

As the ranks of military men and civil fathers moved onward, all eyes were turned towards the point where the minister was seen to approach among them. The shout died into a murmur, as one portion of the crowd after another obtained a glimpse of him. How feeble and pale he looked, amid all his triumph! The energy — or say, rather, the inspiration which had held him up until he should have delivered the sacred message that brought its own strength along with it from Heaven — was withdrawn, now that it had so faithfully performed its office. The glow, which they had just before beheld burning on his cheek, was extinguished, like a flame that sinks down hopelessly among the late-decaying embers. It seemed hardly the face of a man alive, with such a deathlike hue; it was hardly a man with life in him that tottered on his path so nervelessly, yet tottered, and did not fall! 

在军人和文官前进时，所有的目光都转向了牧师在队伍里走来的方向。当一拨又一拨的人群看到了他时，欢呼声减弱成了低语声。在取得的所有胜利中，他看起来是多么虚弱苍白！早先他身上支撑他传达神圣福音的精力，或者更确切地说，福音本身从天上带来的神的灵感，在他恪尽职守之后，就被撤回了。人们刚刚还在他脸上看到的红光，像一团绝望的火焰，在渐渐熄灭的灰烬中黯淡了下去。他面如死灰，简直不像一张活人的脸。他几乎也不像一个生命尚存的人，沿路无力紧张地踉跄而来，摇摇晃晃，却居然没有倒下！
